ParlaMint-AT_2010-05-20-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00067_d7e4441	Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_2010-05-20-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00067_d7e4456	Ladies and gentlemen, you can be calm, the Austrians can be calmed down: The Austrian Federal Army can fulfill its 100 percent tasks. We have been assigned several tasks in a constitutional and simple legal manner. This is the theoretical case of military national defense. This is disaster protection, and this is foreign intervention. We also have to monitor sensitive institutions in Austria and also, in some cases, to contribute to internal security measures. The Austrian Federal Army is well equipped for these tasks. For example, in the field of airspace surveillance, we have fulfilled our 100 percent tasks and can also carry out the airspace monitoring 100 percent with the device that was purchased – not by me, but by the previous Federal Government – with the Eurofighter. We are well equipped for the use of disasters. We are well equipped for foreign use. We have over 1,000 soldiers in foreign service. This means, for example, that we have more soldiers abroad from the population, compared to the Federal Republic of Germany. Thus, the Austrian federal army is well equipped for the tasks of the next few years despite a wave of savings, which does not come from the German army, but from an economic crisis that has spread worldwide, which is also overshadowed in Austria.

Consumers also suffer enormous damage. Back to damage to companies. - I've been researching that. Worldwide, more than €300 billion of piracy is earned, and 300 000 jobs have already been lost due to these growths. Consumers have a huge problem in that they in many cases can no longer distinguish whether they buy an original product or a plagiarized product. That's the problem. There are also enormous safety problems, especially when it comes to drugs. In this area we have experienced a huge explosion of product piracy in recent years. It is not only a financial but also a health damage that can occur in the case of drugs. By reading through the report, it is recognized that a certain helplessness prevails that this problem is not to be addressed accordingly. You have to imagine this; I have also spoken with some customs officials. It is becoming increasingly difficult to see if it is a forgery. That is to say, it takes a very complex procedure for the customs official to find out if this is a forgery. If so, the rightholder must be used; this is a very complex procedure. However, according to the applicable legal situation – and this also confirms this report – 60 percent of the goods are recovered. You have to imagine that! 60 percent of the goods that are laboriously sorted out are then placed on the market again. That's the problem. Now I ask you: Is it surprising that the motivation of officials decreases when their efforts are rewarded in the way that only 40 percent of seized goods are withdrawn from the market in the long term? That is why this report is also a wake-up call for the Ministry of Finance. We have also tabled a request which I would like to briefly explain. First of all, we need a new state of play in the Financial Penal Code so that the whole thing doesn't remain a private debt offence, but becomes an official offence. That means that the state itself becomes active to stop these things. However, we must also take action against the counterfeiting within the EU, because here the customs authority has no control. What is also quite, very important: In connection with drugs, we have to write every counterfeit that is associated as our own crime record in the criminal code, because only in this way can we prevent more people from getting hurt. [[BZÖ's condolences.]] We have experienced a lot of cases in the past that people have suffered serious damage from counterfeit drugs. Even deaths have come through fake drugs that have been transported with mercury and all possible. These are measures that we need to implement in Austria. In other words, we need to do more here in Austria, Mr. Lopatka, the Secretary of State for Finance, to appeal to you, in order to prevent counterfeit goods coming to Austria. But we need to do another thing, namely to ensure that some countries such as China or India finally comply with the rules of the game. We must ensure that these countries are taken to the kandare, because it cannot be that an Austrian company invests millions to develop a new product – company Doublemayr, you know the example – the Chinese then copy this ren, “Doublemayr” and the company Doppelmayr, an Austrian parade company, are making international competition with their own product, so to speak. That can't be, we have to stop that. [[In the case of BZÖ and OVP representatives and the Agg. But it is not only about medicines or foods that can be dangerous, but also about products such as children's toys. Children's toys, which are fake, where there are no target breakers, are easy to buy on markets, not just over the Internet. In this context, one simply has to make it clear that product piracy in this form is not a cavalier crime. It is not a cavalier's crime to reproduce products in this form, to falsify them. Consumers are faced with the loss of quality, but also with the abuse of confidence. You buy a product, you rely on the functionality, on the quality and then you don't get it. The next level, of course, are the companies – the companies that have massive sales losses but also image losses. If products that do not meet expectations, that also means something for the brand. This is the case, for example, with the motorcycle and bicycle manufacturer KTM in Mattighofen in Upper Austria. There, products found at fairs were rebuilt, so to speak, in professional sales, and it was quite clear that there are defective products that pose a great threat. This, of course, is not only contrary to the safety need, but also quite clearly to the rules of competition. If you look at it nationally, then there is the assessment by economic organisations that worldwide 5 to 9 percent of trade in counterfeits is now operating and an estimated volume of €450 billion, which de facto represents the two million jobs. While estimates for Germany are now not directly transferable to Austria in this form, only that you also have a term what that might mean, I give this example: For Germany, says the German Chamber of Commerce, it is about 70 000 jobs that are lost and this results in a damage of about €30 billion. Not to be forgotten is something which is not very unusual, namely the import of fake cigarettes. This is a loss of tax revenue faced by the State. A container of fake cigarettes is about €1.3 million in the state bank. So a number of arguments that clearly show that there is a need for action here. Mr State Secretary, we see progress in this report 2009. We consider EU-wide cooperation on this issue to be an important step, including the EU Customs Action Plan 2009 to 2012. We Greens will agree to the report in this form, although we believe that there are also points that need to be further driven forward. This is public work, but it is essential for the network to be forced. Works aimed at clarifying, preventing and dealing with counterfeit trade marks. Mr State Secretary, I have a request to you. There will be an evaluation of the mid-2010 Action Plan. We would ask you to bring this report, this evaluation also to the Committee, to discuss it so that we can draw the right conclusions, as can be done more efficiently in this form, to ensure the protection of consumers, the protection of businesses, including in the service of the economy. Ikrath: This applies to private!]] They give banks the opportunity to interact with their customers, under a gentle pressure to get them to leave existing foreign currency loans, to switch to euro financing, which brings the banks, under the outline, about €1.6 billion in additional income, to interest. What the banks and whatever you don't say about it is that there is a currency risk on the one hand – that's no question! – but on the other hand, the interest rate advantage is so serious that it is not communicated by the banks to the customer. I'll tell you a current case: Five years ago, a bank loan of 800,000 Swiss francs was received with a interest rate of just over one percent. And now the bank has pointed out, under gentle pressure, that it is now changing to the euro, because at present one would have only a loss of €15 000, but if one had to change only in three or four months and the franc remained so stable and the euro you trust in continued to fall, then the loss would probably be much higher. The Bank thus speaks of €15 000 loss that would be borne if you changed to the euro now, and points out that you would pay much more later on. However, what the bank does not say about this is that in the five years run-up up to now, one has accumulated an interest rate advantage of € 47 000. In other words, the franc can continue to rise or the euro will continue to fall, however, the customer will still have the opportunity to consider for one or one and a half years whether to make a changeover or not. I mean, if you're already bringing in such laws here and forcing people to change to something they might not want, then you should say the whole truth. And now I come to speak on yesterday's debate, and I begin by quoting Mrs Tamandl. She was here yesterday expressly warning of foreign currency credits. So if you already warn about foreign currency loans, you admit at the same time – and you did not do that yesterday – that you have absolutely no confidence in the euro! [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] This means that you indirectly admit that the entire aid package of €750 billion, which the EU has decided on, and in which Austria with €15 billion of liability plus €6 billion of emergency payment will not benefit from, and of which the Greek population will not see a cent, will not benefit because the euro will continue to fall, otherwise you would have to say the honesty and fairness half, the euro is so strong, the European Union is so strong, the Austrian Parliament is so strong, you must not be absolutely afraid to opt out in foreign currency loans if you have an interest rate advantage that has been “washed”. If you are already having a debate in this direction, please tell the whole truth and not just half!

The main issue is the fight against money laundering and terrorist financing. To this end, a package has been chained, which is now available for discussion and decision-making. The aim of this package is to combat money laundering and terrorist financing even more effectively. Money laundering means that funds, income, proceeds from criminal offences are transferred into the regular economic cycle. It has now become a huge global business area. Money laundering is particularly a major problem because it is an important incentive for criminal organisations In the case of criminal associations, for criminals as a whole, by means of criminal machinations, proceeds. But that is only interesting for them if it is possible to introduce these revenues in some form into the regular economic and monetary cycle. The fight against money laundering is therefore a very central and important element in the fight against crime, in particular organised crime. Fighting the financing of terrorism is also a hot issue because international terrorism lives on the fact that billions are being postponed or used to finance terrorist crimes. And now, in various areas – in the area of justice, in the financial market and also in economic legislation – we are taking measures that are suitable to prevent and combat money laundering and terrorist financing even more efficiently. I would like to look in particular at the points concerning justice, the measures we are taking in the area of judicial legislation – they have already been discussed in the judicial committee – and briefly outline or present them in the following minutes. These measures are based on two pillars: On the one hand, we have defined even more clearly the obligations, due diligence obligations of the two legal professions lawyers and notary. One must know that even now there are provisions in the respective laws that impose certain care obligations on these two professions. This is important because, in my opinion, the extremely important obligations of confidentiality of these two professions on the other hand can be a special attraction, a special incentive for criminals, for criminal organizations, for transactions in lawyers and for notaries to move money and to clean money. This is not new, but it is made clear that there is a special duty of care in complex transactions, in complex transactions. I believe it is right and thank God that certain countries should be included in a regulation, ensuring that they are legally secure by showing special care to clients from certain countries. In addition, we have created – almost as a second pillar – additional investigative possibilities under the criminal code. This point was also discussed very long and very intensively in the Committee on Justice, because, of course, it is important, on the one hand, to clearly regulate the restriction of important rights, as well as banking secrecy, for the purpose of identifying criminal offenders, and on the other hand – and here we agree – to preserve banking secrecy. In Austria, there is the banking secret God is grateful at a very high level, but it should not serve to protect offenders and hinder the investigation of crimes. That is why I believe that a good, balanced way has been found to prevent, to clarify, to prevent, to prevent, and to continue to do enough on the other hand the safeguarding of important rights, including banking secrecy. Thank you.

Austria was therefore also included in the OECD's grey list. Austria has now been deleted from this list, but in the previous year the international organisation for the fight against money laundering and terrorist financing identified some shortcomings in the Austrian legal situation. For example, it was criticised that own money laundering in Austria is not a criminal offence. This situation will be substantially improved by the Federal Law to be adopted today, which will amend the legal order, the Notarial Code, the Criminal Code and the Criminal Procedures Regulations in 1975. Money laundering is not an abstract. We have seen this in my federal state – the Board of Management of Hypo Alpe-Adria, Dr. Wolfgang Kulterer, will not be spared the accusation of money laundering. The legislative amendment to be adopted today guarantees that the economic location of Austria does not cause any disadvantage. Why? – If the changes were not made, Austria would find itself on the list published by the Financial Action Task Force, which lists all those countries whose legislation and measures against money laundering do not comply with international standards. This would be fatal, especially for the economic world, but above all for the United States. For example, Austria remains attractive to the international economic world, which is also good, and it should become unattractive for all those who want to misuse the Austrian financial centre to operate money laundering or terrorist financing. Despite these measures, it remains to be feared that criminal energy and, above all, fantasy will not be reduced. But it should become a little harder, a little stoner, and above all it should become more expensive. This progress cannot be underestimated. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-05-20-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00067_d7e12055	Next speaker: Mrs Hakel. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2010-05-20-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00067_d7e12076	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! Mr State Secretary! Mrs. State Secretary! As the last speaker on this item on the agenda, I would like to sum up briefly or explain what money laundering is, because it is obvious that this has not yet arrived at all here. Money laundering is a very clear name for the process of making money out of dirty – i.e. illegal – shops saloonable. Money laundering is a process through which criminals try to hide the true origin of and ownership of the income of criminal activities from the authorities. If they are successful, this allows them to keep control of these incomes while this process is in place and finally to spread the cover of legitimacy over them. In contrast to money laundering – I would like to note – financial resources for terrorist financing could also come from legal sources. The fight against money laundering in Austria – as in most countries – is a complex issue in which many authorities work together. The central objective for all is, of course, to prevent the abuse of the Austrian financial system in order to hide and shift funds from illegal sources. Of course, Austria has so far had a comprehensive, comprehensive and well-functioning system to combat money laundering and terrorist financing. However, there are still some shortcomings that we will now eliminate as a result of the change in the trade regulations, of the economic loyalty law, so that Austria's economic location is protected even better from an abuse by criminals in the future. There will finally be uniform measures which require all members of the economic loyalty profession and the accounting professions to pay increased attention in the future when dealing with persons or companies from countries where there is an increased risk of money laundering and terrorist financing. And the authorities will be able to investigate money laundering suspicions in the future without having to start criminal proceedings immediately. So you can avoid getting criminals warned early. They shall also be obliged to comply with a risk-adequate due diligence obligation in the case of reasons for business relationships, in the handling of transactions, in the suspicion of money laundering or terrorist financing, and in the event of doubts about the authenticity of customer identification data. Even the slightest suspicion of money laundering must be reported to the money laundering reporting office in the Federal Criminal Office. Of course, information on notifications made must not be passed on. Finally, I would like to say the following: It is not new for companies to take care of their business relationships. That's what's happened so far. Therefore, decent merchants with this package will certainly have no problem. Thank you.

Integrative components have been improved. Working hours may also be adjusted according to the health status of young people. Over-company training facilities receive, as in the economy, in the economic sector, appropriate youthful trustees, who can represent their interests there. The equal treatment of foreign training institutions – of course, should depend on the appropriate equivalence of our professions – should be recognized, but only if our recognition abroad is respected accordingly. Education mobility is forced and upgraded by the maximum recognition in the course years from four to six months. Overall, I believe that the dual vocational training system in Austria is a real success story. As Mr Höbart said earlier that we could not accommodate 50,000 apprentices: The concrete figures as of April 2010 are available to me: At the end of April we have 4 398 job seekers at currently 2 935 open apprenticeships. So there is a difference of 1,463; these are those who are not currently staying. This is, for example, much less compared to the figures at the end of December 2009; at that time it was still 2,487 who were not accommodated. I believe that the economy in particular has once again fulfilled its responsibility. Even in difficult times, our training firms train apprentices. A total of 122 965 apprentices are in training as of April 2010, even 0.5 percent more than in the previous year. The largest trainer is the business and craft sector with 54,441 trainees. I believe that the economy is once again showing that it is aware of its responsibility and that it is fair for it to put everything into training, because it is simply important to train the relevant skilled workers over and over again. On the other hand, however, we should also support training firms accordingly. The apprenticeship bonus is an important step in this direction. On the other hand, we should also try to ensure that young people who have already completed the Matura also have opportunities following the Matura in order to be able to do a corresponding training. This would be very useful for many and could be very useful for further career choices. Only monetary possibilities were needed here, and there the AMS is required to support this from its area accordingly. - Thank you.

First of all, I come to the government template which regulates an improvement in social security between the Republic of Austria and Korea. This is mainly the area of pension insurance. It shall ensure that insurance periods are aggregated, that periods of insurance are taken into account, and that performance exports are also regulated. In particular, I would like to note that the agreement also includes a regulation that counteracts double insurance, which has so far been possible by both workers and companies. In other words, it is also ensured that both Austrian companies with branches in Korea and vice versa only make an insurance contribution more each time. The second area deals with Australia. Here I can say that the common agreement was already agreed in 1992. It was changed and improved in 2001. This common agreement mainly regulates the situation of Austrians who have emigrated to Australia in the past, or of those who return from Australia and are now in a situation where they are at an age when they have pension entitlements. There were some ambiguities in the past. Therefore, for the first time, the exemption from insurance rules has been set out in this Agreement, since Australia was the only State with which there was a social security agreement, but which did not regulate the insurance obligation of cross-border persons. Another very important point in this Convention concerns a data protection regime, namely that those data transferred cross-border are actually used only for the purpose of social security, for which calculations relating to, for example, the pension are used. The other two social agreements, both with the United Nations and with UNIDO, also include improvements to pension law. I should like to thank you once again. This will ensure that a special regulation has been adopted for a group of very special workers. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-05-20-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00067_d7e18471	Next speaker: Mr Keck. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2010-05-20-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00067_d7e18492	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Australia is not only a very far away and ever more popular holiday country, but it is also an ever-increasing magnet for those looking for professional adventures or for a career in the distance. Australia is also a classic country of immigration. The doors to Australia are open to everyone, and of course so are many Austrians. Many use this as part of their work on multinational companies. Others go to Australia on their own. To date, the Austrian state has supported these citizens in the form of a bilateral agreement with Australia. For example, pension entitlements have been regulated so that Australian insurance periods in Austria can be maintained legally and Austrian periods for Australian pensions can also be used. The second agreement, which is to be reached today, concerns the obligation of insurance. So far, it has been a duty for those people who, for example, worked in the Australian branches of an Austrian company, both in Australia and in Austria to make their contributions to social security. In the opposite case, of course, it was the same. From now on, this double load should be eliminated. Only more decisive is the State in which the work actually takes place. An important ancillary agreement has also been reached. This is the handling of sensitive data in the processing of these services. A data protection scheme could be found to ensure that all data transmitted across the border is used exclusively for social security purposes and that in no case can such data be misused or lost. The agreement of the Agreement, in the case of changes to national law and in the case of changes to national government structures to adapt it regularly, will also make this right easy to enforce in the future. We are thus giving our citizens legal certainty in the distance and an important reserve, which is always to be welcomed.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-05-20-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00067_d7e18968	Mr President. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) We have already heard, this item on the agenda deals with a number of requests. I would like to confine myself to two requests: on the one hand to the five-party request concerning the definition of service and signal dogs and on the other hand to the compensation payments for counter-passes. The first request is that we should develop uniform definitions for these specifically trained dogs – analogous to the blind dogs regulated by the Federal Disability Act – and we should develop quality criteria for the training of these service and signal dogs, and we should find uniform guidelines, because this is currently in national competence. This proposal, which is probably known by most of our colleagues, presupposes a citizens' initiative, which highlighted the difficulties in this area and also highlighted the urgent need of these dogs for people with disabilities. Service dogs are dogs that benefit mainly people with disabilities and seizures. Signal dogs help deaf and hearing impaired people as well as attacking people in dealing with their daily lives. Since 1999, blind dogs have been defined in § 39a of the Federal Disability Act and are also regulated by law. Unfortunately, for signal and service dogs, this regulation is not yet available. There is currently only one decree from the Federal Ministry that allows for an additional entry into the Federal Disability Pass. Now it is precisely the goal to equalize the service and signal dogs to the blind guide dogs. I am particularly pleased that the request – which will be decided together with all the parties – states that the new regulation should take place with the involvement of people with disabilities, that the countries should be involved, and that experts in particular should be heard and involved. I think it is very important that the implementation of this motion for a resolution should focus on the interests of the people concerned, especially the quality of training, because it is really important for the people concerned to get these service and signal dogs best trained in order to enable the best possible participation in social life. The second request, on which I would like to speak briefly, is the request on compensation for counter-passes. There is indeed a request from Mr Hofer to include this scheme in the right of social compensation. I also know that this is a very, very important and important concern to those concerned, because I have been in contact with these people for many months. We had an appointment together with Federal Minister Stöger, in which we were then signaled that there would be a solution. They all know that in the previous year there was a change to the Contemplation Act, which allows people who have already been rejected to submit a request again and to receive pension benefits, capital compensation and special payments through this Contegration Act – if they have been recognised as victims. Now there is the proposal from the Ministry of Health to pay around € 2.8 million in one-off payments to the people concerned. I think it is a first correct and very important step – which the counter-passers see in a way that can be paid € 2.8 million. Unfortunately, we cannot support the request you have made today. However, there is a request from Mr Huainigg and me that these €2.8 million should be paid out very quickly. I think that it simply has to be preceded by the fact that, in the fastest possible way, the assessments will be concluded, that the compensation and payments can still be made this year, and that, above all, those concerned will not have any disadvantages in paying out these one-off payments – I mean that they will be charged with any social benefits already available – or that they will not be taxed by them. I would really like to thank Mr Stöger for the fact that these one-off payments will be made, because I know that it is a great concern for people and can therefore be made an important contribution by the Republic of Austria.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-05-20-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00067_d7e19391	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! Unfortunately, in the last Committee, my request for family-oriented rehabilitation was treated negatively. As you know, the aftercare of sick children and young people in Austria is far from barrier-free. In addition to competence, the acquisition of costs is in many cases opaque. Even more problematic is the situation of affected families whose children need to use stationary rehabilitation measures. It is not clear whether the Federal Government or the Länder are responsible or whether social security, disability or social assistance bear the costs. In addition, when a reha decision is made, children and their relatives need to avoid to Germany, because there are no comparable offers in Austria. Although there are a few child-friendly reha beds, they do not cover all disease patterns. Children are not small adults, and not even if they are sick. Accommodation in rehabilitation facilities for adults is not targeted. Scientific studies confirm that extreme stress to families with severely ill children often leads to mental and physical disorders of young patients, parents and siblings. But what about us in Austria? – Parents who want to stay with their sick children remain on their own resources and private initiatives ==References== Some of them use family-oriented rehabilitation in Germany. However, this is a lottery game, because whether or for whom it is paid or approved depends on the goodwill of the health authorities and the health insurance companies. Although there is a recommendation from the main association of social security funds, which is that the health insurance funds should pay the costs for the child and a parent and the respective federal state for the other family members, however, various health insurance schemes pay very different cost contributions depending on the federal state, from the full cost acceptance for the whole family to the final decisions on a cost participation. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! In view of the suffering of families with seriously ill children and young people, I am incomprehensible that the Committee has rejected my request. And, Mr Federal Minister, the reference to the forthcoming new study is more than poor. The financing of a family-oriented rehabilitation does not cost any wealth, but brings a lot to the affected. This money must be there because we can also afford to send billions of euros to Greece. I can therefore appeal to your reason and humanity and urge you to finally act for the sick children and their parents in Austria. Thank you.

Imagine you turn on the TV and see a picture, but don't hear anything. This is how many deaf people go, and they can only track the program with subtitles. Or imagine, you turn on the TV, watch a crime, and suddenly the picture falls out, just when it gets exciting and the murderer approaches the victim, and you just hear more music and you don't know what's happening now. This is how blind people, who also watch TV and who only know with explanation aids, the audio description, what really happens. Or you probably know the situation when you open a document in a program and suddenly you only see paragraphs and hieroglyphs, any characters that are not readable. This is how blind people are when they surf the Internet with reading programs, for example on the ORF on-page, the information platform of the ORF, where they simply cannot read this page. Here, we need a accessibility of the ORF. In recent years, disabled organisations have tried to reach them in conciliation procedures. The ORF has only made this very hesitant or not at all, and it is here very far back to other public-law channels. And the argument was always: That was not prescribed by the legislator! That's why I appeal to you for us to do this now. This is now the opportunity to set a stage plan in the ORF Act, which the ORF must implement for the production of accessibility, also with a time-limit, that is to say that in 2015, the ORF – like all other areas of the Federal Disability Equality Act – must provide its customers with accessibility, and that one also determines that the entire program of ORF 1 and ORF 2 must be subtitled, as already the BBC, France and Ireland, for example, and are on this path, or that audio writing needs to be expanded, and that the homepage must be made barrier-free. Of course, this is where the cost argument comes: What's all this cost? – It must be seen that the ORF has a total budget of almost €1 billion and that we will now decide that the ORF will receive €160 million in tax resources. According to the relevant department in the ORF, accessibility would account for 1.5 percent of this tax subsidy, which is €2.4 million. This should be really feasible and is not in relation to the other costs and expenses at all. I appeal to you for an agreement to be reached in Parliament on this. It would have to be all the parties to do so. And apologise, Minister of Social Affairs, for addressing this issue, although it is not discussed in the Committee on Social Affairs, but you may also be able to influence colleagues. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-05-20-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00067_d7e20861	Mr. Neubauer, you always confuse apples with pears. This concept is about the practical learning of all the social activities and also about the personal clarification of the question: Am I at all appropriate to take up a job in the social, health or care sectors in the future? In addition, we are of course also concerned with the social commitment which should be the focus. For more than four years now, the Ministry of Social Affairs has provided a substitute for the withdrawal of the family allowance amounting to €150 for participants who are socially involved for more than ten months on the basis of a 40-hour week. But that is not enough, this amount will be assessed as both social security law and tax law, which, in the end, has logically also led to the fact that the previous costs due to these additional wage costs have increased by over €200 000 to a legendary amount of as much as €836 000. But I would like to point out – and this is fair to say – that the current special directive expires in 2010, which is why the Ministry of Social Affairs is working intensively on an improved solution and an extension. Everyone who knows the Federal Minister Hundstorfer knows what Elan and what seriousness he is dealing with this matter with. In this draft there are truly tangible points, as a modern voluntary law should look like, among other things, the precise definition of what we understand as voluntary activity, right up to the question of anchoring the Austrian Voluntary Council, but also the creation of a service exemption and a payment claim. In the future, however, it will also be necessary to explain who will be allowed to offer such services to voluntary social services and under what quality criteria. Unfortunately, it has not yet been possible to convince the Minister responsible Mitterlehner of the positive content of this draft. Unfortunately, he has not yet given his approval for cost reasons. However, I think that the primary objective of implementation should be to enable young people to experience practical experience in the training and vocational orientation phase. We are therefore called upon to address implementation as soon as possible.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-05-20-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00067_d7e20937	Mr President. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! Since 1968, the voluntary social year has existed in Austria. Innumerable young people have made themselves available free of charge and voluntarily for the purpose of promoting the general public in order to be really active in the social field, and thus, of course, also support the social institutions. At present, it is still de facto that this is not considered to be an education, that family support is therefore not paid further – that there is therefore a cut in family support – and that pocket money and contribution are paid through the institutional administration. That is 43 years of experience, 43 years of many young people and institutions who have really done great things here, and 43 years in which a legal regulation de facto is simply missing. I only partially understand the argument why Minister Mitterlehner is blocking here. Apart from the many decades of experience, it is so that there is a draft, and in the draft – stand 28. 1. 2010 – it is also noted that this federal law with 1. In July 2010, the Law on the Promotion of Framework Conditions for a Voluntary Social Year was to enter into force. That's a fact! A voluntary social year is an engagement of young people who are interested in it and who also want to look and try out whether this social profession is something for them. Many who have made a voluntary social year go to social work. Many who have made a voluntary ecological year go into fields of environmental protection, for example in water protection. That is why the voluntary ecological year and the voluntary social year must be achieved together in a draft.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-05-20-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00067_d7e22907	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! The Minister referred to the crime victim law by saying: Under the Crime Sacrifice Act, children and young people who have experienced violence can be paid for therapy. If you look closely at the crime victim law, that's not quite true. The Crime Victims Act works in such a way that an advertisement must be returned that an illegal and intentional act has been committed, which is threatened with more than 6 months of imprisonment, and has just suffered a health damage. But the benefit is paid only if, first, the therapist or therapist has a health insurance contract and secondly, the claim for damages has not been waived in the proceedings. These are the two major problems that make it so difficult to take treatment with the VOG. An example: A mother notices that her minor daughter has experienced sexual violence and has been abused, and repays ad. The culprit is the father. Unfortunately, this is happening in Austria. The daughter must be in therapy. The mother knows this, the care facilities know that the social worker care facility in which the child is located – and the child also gets a treatment site quite quickly. The only problem is that the mother refuses the statement that she is in the proceedings – and thus the child no longer has a legal claim to receive this therapy through the crime victim law. This means that the costs have to be reimbursed, and this also means that there is no more support. For example, two would be if no notice was returned. We all know, especially from social work with children and young people, from the medical field working with children and young people, that it is extremely difficult to repay ads at all. Reimbursement against the father or against the known ad, mostly by the mother – the children themselves, who are underage, will not often refund ad – is extremely difficult. In this case, there is no cost-taking either. So, and as a third problem, after the missing therapists with health insurance contracts, after the claims for compensation, we still have the blem, that children who are indirectly affected by violence, namely all those children who see in the family household how the dad beats mom, experience the traumatic experiences at home, are not encouraged by the crime victim law and do not receive therapy. That's the third problem. We know that all children and young people who are affected by sexual violence must be given all the opportunity to develop their traumatic experiences. This means that there is really someone who accompanies and cares for them until it is partially over and until they can enter into normal relationships themselves, themselves can also lead a normal life and take on responsibility for their own lives. The crime victim law is not a solution here and, in my opinion, it is the completely wrong argument. We know that the white ring, the gull, the child protection center, but also the child health league agree that we have too few treatment places in Austria. Unfortunately, there are still queues, even for those affected by violence. There is too little resources for data collection. In other words, all these institutions cannot say: Which children are in the system that have experienced violence? And: Where were they given if, for example, a treatment site could not be provided? Where, then, are these children going, in which system are they inside? That means waiting times are preprogrammed. In other words, Austria needs treatment places for all affected children and young people, and that means that either the crime victim law needs to be modernised or a new bill comes in here, which really helps all children and young people who have had to experience such a traumatic experience.

The additive subject of Geriatry, for which a completed concept and also a curriculum has existed for a long time, has been repeatedly postponed in the past. Here it should be down In this important area, the doctors and general physicians are allowed to make their additional training available. Nor is there a specialist training for general medicine, which is already a reality in 13 EU countries, which is unfortunately not yet available in Austria. We think it would be reasonable to start thinking about how this could be done better in the future in the field of medical study. I think it would be a step forward if we were to take over the German model, where parts of the medical education are already incorporated into the course, into the curriculum, and after studying, after a year of practical work can actually be worked independently, as an adopted doctor, and as a counter-business, so to speak, if you want to introduce the specialist training for Geriatrics, which, however, takes five years instead of today, where the gym only lasts three years. But I believe that this is in the interests of the quality of training and also of the older Austrians. Thank you.

A few comments on the application concerning the occupational images of industrial masseur and massage. The profession of the medical massager includes activities which fall within the field of human medicine and are therefore reserved for the statutory health profession. Vocational training requires a medical arrangement. The profession of industrial masseurs serves to treat healthy people as wellness or well-being treatment. These are not allowed to treat sick people. It makes sense to separate between health-necessary treatment and wellness when it comes to whether you have to or want to do something. It is important to distinguish whether one objectively improves health or increases the subjective well-being. One is an insurance-related benefit, the other is not. Contrary to the observations made in the present application, the permeability of industrial masseur to the massage of medicinal products is given even after the expiry of the transitional provisions. We are only required to ensure that this training remains financially viable. Training and continuing vocational training must not be a question of the wallet, but must be a question of skill and motivation. It should not be mentioned that a lot of money must be earned at the interface between wellness and health. That will also be the motivation of various discussions. It is important to have clear guidelines on the orientation of patients and customers so that they can also come into the right hands. It is currently underway an evaluation of the law on curative masseurs. We therefore see no need at present to support this proposal.

Point First reading: Request by Mrs Carmen Gartelgruber, colleagues concerning a federal law with which the Federal Law on the granting of advances on the maintenance of children (Content Provision Act 1985 – UVG), BGBl. No. 451/1985, amended (1020/A)
ParlaMint-AT_2010-05-20-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00067_d7e28689	We'll get to 48. Agenda item. Mrs Gartelgruber is speaking on behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2010-05-20-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00067_d7e28714	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! After discussions about football playing again on a serious topic. In my request, I am dealing with a change in the granting of advances on the maintenance of children. [[Unrest in the hall.]] Imagine that you are divorced, have two children and expect your ex-partner's support. However, this is not transferred; You have to wait for it. Of course, the monthly payment obligations do not fail. Apartment, electricity, heating and so-called “banale” things like food and clothing, especially for the children, must be paid. - Actually, no one listens to me. I don't think this is a subject that anyone is interested in now. Is a madman, eerie! [[Demonstrative applause with the FPÖ and the Abg. Hagenhofer.]] . I would like to deal with a serious issue here, but the ladies and gentlemen have nothing more to do than kick. Further in my actual speech: In order to get a maintenance advance from the state, an enforceable execution title is required – and this can take time when you know the “speed” of the courts in Austria. In this case, sufferers are the single parents and their children, and it may just happen that single parents have a massive financial disadvantage. It is clear to me, of course, that the public hand cannot intervene everywhere, in any emergency situation and that there is always a certain risk of abuse in the area of social benefits, but in this case in particular, there must be a solution, as the law in force has simply shown weaknesses in practice. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, there is an urgent need for action. Anyone who knows the numbers and knows how precarious the financial management of everyday life is in the lives of single parents also knows that something has to be done here. Especially the proportion of working poor among single parents is very high. But we are not talking here about relinquishing a second car or a holiday, but about a fixed shortage. The tax relief for families – e.g. child allowance or tax deductibility of child care costs – does not benefit single parents because their income is usually too low. We must not adhere to rigid regulations, especially with regard to children and their needs. It cannot be that the provision of children is no longer guaranteed only because an enforceable execution order cannot be issued. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] We liberals are sincerely trying to remedy this – and therefore I very much welcome the fact that a survey on care and maintenance will be held at the end of June. The first important step has now been taken, and I urge you to remove the formal obstacles to obtaining a maintenance advance and, so to speak, to cut down the standards with regard to the required execution title. [[I should like to congratulate Mr Rosenkranz.]] I can ask you all to go the way with us. The affected children and their parents, and especially those women who are not spoiled by life, will thank you.
